A Cheaper City Weekend – Stay Near a Train Line and Do the Pass Math

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Many or all of the credit card, banking, and other offers on this site are from companies that pay us. For example, we may be paid when you click a link, are approved for a product, or open an account. Compensation may influence which products we cover and how, where, and in what order they appear. It doesn’t influence our editorial evaluations, ratings, or opinions, which are ours alone. We don’t include every bank, credit card company, or offer available in the marketplace.

On a city weekend, book a room near a train line rather than downtown, ride transit instead of paying to park, and plan your days around sights that are free for visitors. Buy an attractions pass only if the places you’ll see would cost more at the gate.

The easy version of a city weekend, a downtown hotel with the car in its garage and a ticket to every big attraction, is also the priciest. In the Loop, Chicago’s downtown, the Palmer House Hilton charges $57 a night just to self-park.

Good transit means you need neither a car nor a downtown room. A hotel near a train or bus stop a few miles out often costs less than one downtown, and so can a hostel’s private room.

Two other savings need a check before you count on them. A museum’s free day may be for local residents only, and an attractions pass saves money only on places you’d have paid to see anyway.

In Chicago, a CTA 3-day pass costs $15 and covers 72 hours of buses and trains, including the Blue Line ride from O’Hare that costs $5 without a pass. Buy it from a vending machine in the O’Hare station. A taxi from O’Hare to downtown averages $50, the airport says. Stay near a Blue Line stop such as Logan Square, and the same train takes you to the Loop.

The Art Institute, the Field Museum, and the Shedd Aquarium save their free days for Illinois residents. Lincoln Park Zoo and the National Museum of Mexican Art are free to everyone, and Chicago Greeter volunteers lead free hour-long walks from the Chicago Cultural Center.

Chicago CityPASS costs $144 per adult for five attractions over nine days, with upgrades such as faster entry at several of them. See just three, such as the Skydeck, the Art Institute, and the Field Museum, and general admission without the upgrades starts at $94 for all three, $50 less than the pass.

He played for Bolton Wanderers and Aston Villa - Then won Celebrity Big Brother

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Former Premier League star and Serbian international Sasa Curcic earned both fame in his home country and a substantial payout after becoming a reality TV star following his retirement from the game.

As professional footballers reach the end of their playing careers, many are left with difficult decisions over what to do with their futures. Even the luckiest of players only make it until their mid-to-late thirties in the professional game, and the question of what to do after playing contracts dry up is a very real one.

For some, coaching or scouting is an option, while for others there is a clear appeal to a post-playing career in the media. By taking these routes, former players can rely on the experience that they picked up over the decades during which they were involved in the game. Others enter into business, being able to invest as a result of their time in the game.

But for some players, what's missing is the roar of the crowd, and in the case of the former Aston Villa, Bolton Wanderers and Crystal Palace star Sasa Curcic, the former player found a whole new audience as a reality TV star once his playing days were over.

Sasa Curcic arrived in England having already impressed in his home city of Belgrade. Having first signed for OFK Beograd in 1991, he moved to Partizan Belgrade two years later, having already made his debut for the FR Yugoslavia — now Serbia & Montenegro — national team at the very start of his professional career.

In the summer of 1995, Bolton Wanderers had just been promoted to the Premier League and were looking to strengthen. Curcic was an obvious talent, so was signed by Bolton’s managerial team of Colin Todd and Roy McFarland as they sought to avoid a quick return to the second-tier.

What are your memories of Sasa Curcic? Let us know in the comments below!

Described by When Saturday Comes as "the greatest thriller at Burnden Park since the iconic Frank Worthington", Curcic was given a free role in the Bolton midfield, but he was unable to keep his team in the Premier League; they were relegated back in bottom place in the table.

But having tasted life in the Premier League with Bolton, Curcic wanted to stay there following the club's relegation, and at the start of the 1996-97 season he left for Aston Villa. It was a disastrous move. Curcic fell out with manager Brian Little, and he left the club in March 1998 for Crystal Palace with just 29 League appearances under his belt. The move from Bolton to Villa was later ranked by The Times as the 12th-worst in the first ten years of the Premier League.

But life at Selhurst Park proved to be no easier for him. He managed just 23 League appearances for Palace before leaving the club in 1999, with his spell in South London best-remembered for his protests against NATO bombing of Serbia while he was playing for Palace. After spells in Major League Soccer and in Scotland with Motherwell, he confirmed his retirement as a player in 2001.

FLW ranks the eight most famous footballers to have played in the Championship at some point in their career.

Having picked up a total of 14 appearances for his national team, Curcic was still well-known in Serbia, and he capitalised on that when he entered the Celebrity Big Brother house for their version of the show in May 2007.

As one of thirteen contestants, Curcic proved that he was still able to get people on his side, and it was confirmed a month later that he'd won the competition, scooping a prize of €50,000 and earning himself some lucrative television commercial work as a result of it.

But fame wouldn't last for him. He left the set of another reality show called Farma — "The Farm" — after just a couple of weeks the following year and faded from view again, eventually resurfacing back in South London in 2015, forming an academy with connections to Crystal Palace. He also had a spell managing, back in his home country. Appointed into the head coach's position at the Belgrade-based club Borac Lazarevac in November 2021, he lasted six months in the position before leaving.

Always a bit of a wild child — Brian Little would later tell a few stories of the midfielder's eccentricities, including "hiring a double-decker booze bus with models for his Aston Villa teammates" — Curcic himself later admitted that moving to England in the first place had been a bit of a mistake: "My dream was to play for Partizan, my team. I should have stayed. I would have my own stand, I’d be manager."

It speaks volumes for the self-confidence that got him to the top of the game in England and his subsequent fall from grace that he continued to believe in the certainty that his career would have ended up so differently had he stayed in Serbia in the first place. And Bolton Wanderers fans of a certain age certainly still remember the role he played following their original return to the Premier League.

GLP-1s Like Ozempic May Boost Brain Health, But Can They Lower Dementia Risk?

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Follow Healthline on our socials for all things health.

GLP-1 medications have transformed obesity and type 2 diabetes treatment. Now, researchers are investigating whether the effects of this blockbuster class of drugs extend beyond weight loss and affect brain health.

A comprehensive new review published on November 5 in the journal Science evaluated the current evidence on how GLP-1 medications like semaglutide and tirzepatide may affect the brain across aging, cardiometabolic disease, and Alzheimer’s disease.

The authors of the review suggested that GLP-1 medications like Ozempic and Zepbound may offer brain health benefits. However, they were also left with the question of whether the medications act directly on the brain or whether better brain health is actually a consequence of improved cardiometabolic health.

Sheena Aurora, MD, vice president, Medical Affairs, Alzheimer’s Association, who wasn’t involved in the review, told Healthline that more research is needed to better understand the effects of GLP-1 drugs on the brain.

“While research suggests GLP-1s could reduce brain inflammation, improve blood vessel health, and even protect neurons, much broader, randomized clinical trials on GLP-1s and brain health, including in people with Alzheimer’s disease and other dementias, are needed,” she said.

GLP-1 medications like semaglutide, the active ingredient in Ozempic and Wegovy, mimic the GLP-1 hormone. Tirzepatide, the active ingredient in Mounjaro and Zepbound, also targets a second hormone pathway called GIP.

These medications have been shown to produce significant weight loss and improvements in cardiometabolic health.

This is important for brain health because obesity, type 2 diabetes, and vascular dysfunction are all linked with cognitive impairment and dementia. Insulin resistance and chronic inflammation may also link metabolic and brain health.

Analyses of large outcome trials and observational cohorts have suggested that GLP-1 medications may be associated with a reduced risk of cognitive impairment and dementia. However, randomized trials in diseases like Alzheimer’s disease have yet to demonstrate clinical benefits.

Around 200 preclinical and mechanistic studies have shown benefits for learning, memory, synaptic plasticity, and neuronal resilience, though results are heterogeneous in design and direction, so a clear consensus on magnitude and mechanism remains lacking.

It is also unclear how well GLP-1 medications reach the brain. They appear to reach certain areas, such as the hypothalamus, brainstem, and circumventricular organs, more readily than regions protected by the blood-brain barrier.

The report authors noted that some studies have shown that GLP-1 receptor agonists may reduce the risk of cognitive impairment and dementia in people with type 2 diabetes. This may be due to cognitive issues being linked to factors such as blood sugar, blood pressure, vascular health, and sleep.

Mir Ali, MD, medical director of MemorialCare Surgical Weight Loss Center at Orange Coast Medical Center in Fountain Valley, CA, who wasn’t involved in the report, told Healthline, “These findings are interesting, but like many of the other benefits associated with GLP-1 medications, a direct causal relationship has not yet been proven.”

“Rather, the benefits appear to be more closely tied to the reduction in obesity and better control of diabetes,” he said.

Aurora noted that growing evidence supports the heart-brain connection.

She cited new findings from the Alzheimer’s Association’s landmark U.S. POINTER study presented at the Clinical Trials in Alzheimer’s Disease (CTAD) conference November 16–19 in Boston, MA.

The research showed that the structured lifestyle intervention program also improved heart health risk factors, such as:

“U.S. POINTER can change those risk factors within 24 months, and better heart health risk-factor control was linked with better cognition,” Aurora said. “We’re also interested to see whether adding a drug like a GLP-1 to a U.S. POINTER-style lifestyle program improves cognition in at-risk older adults more than the program alone.”

To find out, the Alzheimer’s Association is funding the Prevention of Risk for Cognitive Decline through Combined Therapy (PROTECT-Cog) global phase 3 clinical trial.

PROTECT-Cog is a first-of-its-kind trial designed to evaluate whether combining a proven multidomain lifestyle intervention with a metabolism-targeting drug, like a GLP-1, can further reduce the risk of cognitive decline, mild cognitive impairment, and dementia in at-risk older adults.

Dung Trinh, MD, internist at MemorialCare Medical Group and Chief Medical Officer of Healthy Brain Clinic in Irvine, CA, who also wasn’t involved in the review, told Healthline, “I often remind my patients that what’s good for the heart is generally good for the brain.”

“The brain depends on a healthy cardiovascular system to deliver a constant supply of oxygen, glucose, and other nutrients. Conditions such as high blood pressure, diabetes, obesity, and high cholesterol can damage blood vessels, promote chronic inflammation, and increase the risk of stroke and vascular cognitive impairment,” he said.

“Over time, these changes can also contribute to the development or progression of dementia.”

Trinh added that while GLP-1 medications can improve several cardiometabolic risk factors, these improvements do not automatically translate into proven dementia prevention.

“Regular physical activity, a heart-healthy diet, adequate sleep, and management of cardiovascular risk factors remain essential strategies for maintaining brain health as we age,” he said.

When the report authors turned their attention to more stringent tests on semaglutide in people already living with early Alzheimer’s disease, the picture began to change.

Novo Nordisk’s EVOKE and EVOKE + Phase 3 Trials examined the efficacy of semaglutide in people with early stage Alzheimer’s disease.

The studies showed that while semaglutide produced changes in several disease-related biomarkers, it didn’t slow the progression of Alzheimer’s disease.

This emphasizes that fixing metabolic issues might prevent cognitive decline in someone with obesity or diabetes. But it might not be enough to reverse the damage in someone with established Alzheimer’s disease, where profound synaptic loss and protein pathology have already occurred.

The review authors suggest that future research on obesity and diabetes should also include rigorous and specific cognitive testing.

Startup Pitch Competitions Pay Equity-Free Cash – Enter the Ones You Can Win

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Many or all of the credit card, banking, and other offers on this site are from companies that pay us. For example, we may be paid when you click a link, are approved for a product, or open an account. Compensation may influence which products we cover and how, where, and in what order they appear. It doesn’t influence our editorial evaluations, ratings, or opinions, which are ours alone. We don’t include every bank, credit card company, or offer available in the marketplace.

Startup pitch competitions award cash, feedback, and investor introductions without taking a share of your company. Enter contests limited to your industry, region, school, or stage of growth, where the field is small, and skip any whose prize isn’t worth the travel and weeks of preparation. Read the rules first, since a few prizes are investments that take equity.

Universities, cities, corporations, and conferences all run these contests. For a founder too early for a bank loan or an investor’s check, a well-chosen one is among the cheapest sources of money and feedback.

Your odds depend on who else can enter. A contest limited to your school, region, industry, or stage of growth draws from a smaller pool than one open to every startup. MIT’s $100K competition, for one, awards a $100,000 grand prize without taking a share of the company, and its rules admit only teams with a current MIT student.

Grow-NY, a food and agriculture contest in upstate New York, shows what to look for in the rules. Its $1 million top prize comes in stages as the winner hits milestones. The winner gives Cornell University, which runs the contest, either a right to 3% of the company or a promise of future shares. It must also locate or do business in the region for at least 12 months.

Then weigh each prize against what competing costs you: the entry fee, the travel, and the weeks you’ll spend on slides and practice instead of on customers.

Build the pitch around a clear problem, the customer who has it, and early proof that customers want what you sell, such as sales, a trial run, or a waitlist. Cut it to the time limit, look up who’s judging so you can lead with what they care about, and expect questions about your numbers.

Picture a student team that enters three regional contests in one spring. It loses the first, wins $15,000 in the second, and at the third meets an angel investor, someone who invests her own money in startups. A year later, she leads the team’s first funding round.

Win or lose, leave with the judges’ feedback and their contact information. Ask the organizer for your scores and the judges’ comments. Within two days, send each judge who asked a hard question a better answer and a short update on your progress.

Realty Income Pays Dividends Every Month. Here's What You Can Expect $10,000 Invested to Pay Monthly in 2027.

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Realty Income has paid a monthly dividend every month for more than 56 years.

By Jennifer Saibil – Nov 21, 2026 at 6:33PM EST

10 stocks we like better than Realty Income ›

If you're looking for a dividend stock, Realty Income (O +0.64%) is one of the best. It's a real estate investment trust (REIT), a business structure for real estate companies that pays out 90% of income as dividends, which is why REITs are often on best-dividend-stock lists.

Realty Income has an excellent track record, a high yield, and plenty of reasons to be confident about its future success. If you invest $10,000 right now, here's how much you can expect to get in dividends in 2027.

Realty Income has supported its top dividend by running a massive real estate business that leases properties to large essential retailers. These are chains you know and love and likely shop at, like Walmart (WMT +1.51%), Home Depot (HD +0.70%), 7-Eleven, and Dollar General (DG -2.23%).

These are stores with well-established brands and strong cash flow, making them reliable renters. They have multi-year leases, some as long as 20 years, and Realty Income's occupancy rate is a high 98.8%. It has maintained a rate between 97% and 99% since 2000.

Grocery and convenience stores make up almost 20% of the portfolio, providing a resilient revenue stream, but the company has also branched out into other areas as it expands and diversifies. The U.K. is one of its largest markets today, accounting for more than 15% of total properties, and Wynn Resorts (WYNN -2.07%) has become one of its biggest customers. Industrials make up a healthy 17% of the portfolio, and Realty Income is also getting started in data centers, which make up 1%.

The company has more than 15,500 properties in 96 industries, and it's always buying new properties to keep growing. It has a $5.5 billion revolving credit facility, and it's raised more than $2 billion so far this year from various sources. It has a large pipeline of quality properties to choose from, and expanding beyond retail offers it more opportunities.

Realty Income has paid a dividend monthly for the past 677 months, or more than 56 years. It's one of the few dividend stocks that cuts you a check every month.

It also raises its dividend quarterly rather than annually, which is unusual among great dividend stocks. It has raised the dividend for the past 117 quarters, or more than 29 years.

At the current price, the dividend yields 5.6%, an excellent yield for a reliable dividend stock.

Realty Income is a dividend play, and the stock hasn't changed much over the past 10 years. However, now has been a particularly challenging time for the company as interest and mortgage rates remain high.

Since its business model involves borrowing lots of money, higher interest rates mean it has to pay more to service its debt.

While shares are down 7% over the last six months, it doesn’t appear to be in any danger, and it’s flourished across a range of environments for more than 50 years, paying its dividend without missing a monthly payment.

As of its latest raise last week, Realty Income pays $3.288 in annualized dividends, up from $3.258. That's a small change of only 0.9%. If it continues like that for the next four quarters, by the end of 2027, it should be $3.408 in annualized dividends. The average of the two is $3.348, and divided by 12, that's an average monthly payment of $0.279.

If you invest $10,000 today at a share price of $57.66 (at the time of writing), that gets you 173 shares, and next year, you'd get about $48.27 every month.

That may not seem like much, but it's reliable and will continue to rise. It also highlights the need to save for retirement to ensure a livable monthly income.

Johnstown Mill Rats name new general manager

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

JOHNSTOWN, Pa. – The Johnstown Mill Rats announced Megan Semanchik has been named general manager.

Semanchik brings experience in sports sales, corporate partnerships, game-day operations and food and beverage management to the position, including previous roles with both the Mill Rats in the Prospect League and the Johnstown Tomahawks in the North American Hockey League.

"I'm grateful for the opportunity to take on this role and continue growing with the Mill Rats," Semanchik said.

"I've had the opportunity to see the organization from several different perspectives, and I'm excited to build on that experience.

"I'm looking forward to working with our staff, partners and community to continue growing the Mill Rats and creating a great experience at the ballpark."

Semanchik, who replaced 2025 Prospect League general manager of the year Sarah Finlay, began her professional sports career with the Tomahawks as an intern before joining the organization full time in group sales and sponsorships.

Her responsibilities extended during her time with the organization, giving her experience across multiple areas of sports business and operations.

She later joined the Mill Rats as a member of the game-day staff before moving into the role of food and beverage manager.

"We've had the opportunity to watch Megan grow within our organization, and she has consistently shown the initiative, work ethic and leadership we're looking for," said Bart Vickroy, president of Johnstown Family Entertainment, the ownership group of the Mill Rats. "She understands the business side of sports, she knows our organization and she has a strong connection to the Johnstown sports community. We're excited to have Megan take on this role and help lead the Mill Rats into our next chapter."

As general manager, Semanchik will oversee the club's day-to-day business operations, including corporate partnerships, ticket and group sales, marketing, community relations, game-day operations and the overall fan experience.

Semanchik, a Portage Area High School graduate, earned a bachelor's degree in business administration with a minor in sports management from Mount Aloysius College, completing the program in three and a half years. She was a member of the women's volleyball team and served as team manager for the men's volleyball program.

PHOTO GALLERY | 'Amazing feeling': Richland strikes early in repeat 6-2A championship performance

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

ALTOONA, Pa. – Top-seeded and undefeated Mount Union had outscored 12 opponents 515-124 entering Friday night's District 6 Class 2A title game against defending champion Richland.

Rams coach Brandon Bailey and his staff emphasized the importance of grabbing early momentum to keep Mount Union's 2,000-yard rusher Nasir Collins off the field as much as possible.

Two first-quarter interceptions and a pair of successful onside kicks provided Richland a cushion. Junior quarterback Jacob Regan and his talented receivers also did their share in a 56-28 Rams victory.

Richland will face District 10 champion Farrell next week in a PIAA Class 2A quarterfinal.

PHOTO GALLERY | Richland claims District 6 2A championship with 56-28 win over Mount Union

"They're a quality football team and they're really explosive," Bailey said as his 11-2 squad won the program's seventh district crown overall. "We were coming out being very aggressive. We were able to get two onside kicks and a bunch of big plays in the passing game.

"This is a resilient team and they came out to win a title tonight."

Regan completed 23 of 30 passes for 332 yards and five touchdowns. He ran for 45 yards and another score.

"We were able to really execute on offense," said 6-foot-4 senior receiver Arnold Mugerwa, a Rutgers commit. "The passing game was amazing. All of us have been working hard on Tuesdays when we get our wide receivers work in."

Mugerwa caught five passes for 109 yards and touchdowns of 33, 54 and 10 yards. Senior Jamere Christian hauled in 10 receptions for 134 yards, including a 35-yard score.

Junior Alex Bond made five catches for 51 yards, with a 16-yard touchdown, and junior Jackson Feichtel had two receptions for 28 yards.

The Rams also had success rushing against Mount Union (12-1). Senior Grayson Mahla carried 17 times for 110 yards, scoring 22- and 12-yard touchdowns.

"We prepped throughout the week super-hard," Mahla said. "We try to practice so that the practice is the hard part and the game is the easy part.

"We had a great game plan and we took care of our business, controlled what we could control, and came out on top. I'm extremely grateful to be out here with my guys who I love to be with."

Mahla intercepted a Collins pass on the game's opening possession to set up the Rams at the 47-yard line.

Six plays later, Regan ran 13 yards into the end zone, and Bentley Smith kicked the first of his eight extra points at 7:46 of the first quarter.

"That was absolutely huge," Mahla said of scoring first.

Freshman Josh Regan then booted a successful onside kick that took a couple opportunistic bounces before the Rams' Feichtel caught the ball at the Trojans 36-yard line. Jacob Regan hit Mugerwa with a 33-yard scoring pass at 7:12, giving the Rams two touchdowns in 34 seconds of game clock.

"I don't think I've ever been a part of a quicker start to a game," said Mahla, a four-year starter.

"That was absolutely huge. We got the momentum in our favor really quick and that helped us ride out the rest of the game to get a solid win against a really solid team."

Josh Regan's next onside kick caromed off a Mount Union player and landed in the hands of Richland's Dom Shank at the Rams' 49.

Mahla capped the drive on a 22-yard touchdown run at 4:28.

"Both of them came out of the blue. We didn't talk about it all week, but it ended up working out," Josh Regan said of the onside kicks. "My goal is always just to kick it as close to the sidelines as possible at the 50 and it worked out perfect. A little lucky on the second one, but whatever works, works."

Richland's Ryan Rokita intercepted Maddox Hobbs' pass, giving his team possession at the Mount Union 30-yard line.

Jacob Regan passed to Christian, and the Rams reciever dashed to the end zone for a 35-yard touchdown.

"We wanted to be aggressive and get those points early," Bailey said. "No. 11 (Collins) is a special player. They've got a lot of great players.

"Our kids were just able to slow them down just enough and we were able to score a lot of points."

Mount Union finally scored when Hobbs connected with TyJeir Hooper on a 5-yard touchdown pass. Josh Regan blocked the extra-point kick, setting a 28-6 score.

Richland answered quickly, as Jacob Regan and Christian teamed on a 45-yard pass that set up Mahla's 12-yard scoring run at 8:41.

The Rams quarterback and Mugerwa combined on a 54-yard touchdown pass to put Richland 42-6 advantage.

"We were able to get big, explosive plays," Bailey said. "We have some really special wide receivers. Our offensive line did a great job.

"No. 52 (junior Rahmeir Smith) had double-digit sacks coming in. It was a point of emphasis for us to make sure we had time to throw to our guys."

Mount Union added Hooper's 29-yard touchdown reception from Hobbs, who also hit Collins on the two-point conversion pass to set a 41-14 halftime score.

The teams traded scores in the third quarter, before Richland added a fourth-quarter score.

Collins, who entered the game with 2,069 rushing yards and 38 touchdowns, finished with 71 yards and no touchdowns on 13 carries. He passed for 211 yards, two scores and three interceptions.

Richland made its eighth 6-2A title game appearance in the past nine years, and the Rams' 11th title bout in 16 years. Richland won gold in 2012, took four straight titles from 2018-21 and won the past two championships.

"It means a lot going back to back," Mugerwa said. "From my experience, all four years being here, we lost the first two and won these two.

Mike Mastovich is a sports reporter and columnist for The Tribune-Democrat. He can be reached at 814-532-5083. Follow him on Twitter @Masty81.

Building your own mini PC is harder than you would think

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Building a custom PC is a wonderfully satisfying feeling. Even if you've never built one for yourself, it's ingrained itself into the world of content creation and social media so fiercely that everyone has most likely at least watched someone partially build a PC at some point.

As you may have noticed, there are so many different form factors for cases and unique enclosures. One of the more interesting ones that has been carving out its own funky niche in the market is the mini PC build. Because of the unique form factors and limited space these cases allow, they come with challenges that make a build of this type a bit more difficult than normal.

Unlike larger mid- and full-range towers, the mini PC builds tend to focus on a straight-ahead approach with very little wiggle room for frills. Things that we take for granted in larger builds, like room for maximum airflow or a larger GPU, become pertinent struggles in the mini PC world.

One of the core things to understand upfront about mini PC builds is the case itself. What we know as mini PCs tend to be built within Mini-ITX or microATX cases. For as small as they are, you might think they'd cost significantly less than their larger siblings. In a lot of situations, that's true, but Mini-ITX cases also come in a lot of different types. Some of them offer fun bells and whistles, which can run your bill up before you know it.

A lot of the mainstream mini computers that you can buy from companies, like Apple's Mac mini or certain models of the ASUS NUC, are built with an SoC — a system-on-a-chip that combines a CPU with a GPU — and RAM sometimes soldered into the motherboard itself. Doing something like that isn't really feasible, but you can get fairly close with some commercial cases.

No matter which one you're looking at, it may be reassuring to know that despite their small stature, a lot of them are surprisingly roomy. You're still going to be limited by what the case allows in terms of dimensions and compatibility, but those are all easy to contrast and compare with the official specs or using a site like PCPartPicker to determine if a component will fit in your case.

The unorthodox shape and size of a lot of mini PC cases means that while it's entirely possible to fill them with a lot of cool stuff, your approach is going to have to be a bit more economical and predetermined. For most of your situations, a mini ITX case will only accommodate a mini-ITX motherboard, while microATX cases will be able to fit in both Mini-ITX and microATX motherboards due to the micro cases being a bit bigger on average.

Airflow also becomes something of a problem, as the smaller size of these cases means your internal components have a lot less room to breathe. There's usually very little space once all the guts are mashed inside, and that's especially true if you go for a discrete GPU as opposed to an APU that combines the CPU and the GPU. The prices of most small form factor, or SFF, PC components are also a bit steep.

SFF power supplies, motherboards, CPU coolers and GPUs can all contribute a surprising amount to the final price of a machine, which may make going for a standard ATX build more appealing. Finding all these unique-sized parts isn't even the most challenging issue when building a mini PC, as the physical build itself can be far more difficult than that of a standard ATX machine.

There's a lot less room, but you also need to be more careful with the actual installation of parts. A standard ATX build can largely be put together in any order, but mini PC builds need to be put together in a specific order due to the part sizes and clearance concerns.

Rising Rates – Pay Down Variable-Rate Debt First and Move Savings to Higher Yields

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Many or all of the credit card, banking, and other offers on this site are from companies that pay us. For example, we may be paid when you click a link, are approved for a product, or open an account. Compensation may influence which products we cover and how, where, and in what order they appear. It doesn’t influence our editorial evaluations, ratings, or opinions, which are ours alone. We don’t include every bank, credit card company, or offer available in the marketplace.

When the Federal Reserve raises rates, the variable rates on your credit card balances and home equity line of credit climb on their own, but your savings rate rises only if your bank raises it. Put extra payments toward those balances first, and move the cash you’re saving to a high-yield savings account that passes the increase on.

The Fed raised its benchmark rate a quarter point in September, its first increase since July 2023.

A variable rate on a credit card or a home equity line of credit (HELOC) follows an index such as the prime rate, which moves with the Fed’s rate. Card issuers normally can’t raise the rate on a balance you already owe, but a rise in that index is one of the exceptions, the Consumer Financial Protection Bureau notes. On a $10,000 card balance carried all year, each percentage point added to the rate costs about $100 in interest.

Online banks have passed more of each increase on to savers. During the 2022 and 2023 hikes, they raised deposit rates about 0.3 percentage point more than branch-based banks for each full point the Fed added, a National Bureau of Economic Research study found. At an online bank paying 4%, $20,000 earns about $800 a year. At a big bank paying 0.01%, it earns $2.

Pay down card balances first, then a HELOC ahead of any fixed-rate loan charging the same rate or less. A card balance you’ll carry for months can move to a fixed-rate personal loan, or to a 0% balance transfer card you can pay off before the 0% period ends, after you compare the fees. If you’ll borrow for a car or a home soon, lock in a fixed rate before you buy.

Keep your emergency fund in a high-yield savings account. For new certificates of deposit (CDs), stick to terms of a year or less until rates level off, or build a CD ladder so part of your money comes due every six months.

Cash beyond that fund belongs on any card balance you carry. Banks charged an average 22.36% on cards carrying a balance in the third quarter, the Federal Reserve reports, more than five times what a 4% savings account pays.

Find a higher rate for your emergency fund among the best high-yield savings accounts.

Michael Burry Says He's Looking at Netflix Stock in the High $30s. I'd Consider It Now, Well Above That.

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The famous investor is eyeing a price around a third lower. Is it worth waiting for?

By Daniel Sparks – Nov 21, 2026 at 6:22PM EST

Netflix (NFLX -2.96%) has Michael Burry's attention. Just not at today's price.

That's a long way down. Netflix shares closed at $58.46 on Thursday, Nov. 19, their lowest close since May 2024 (using split-adjusted prices, because Netflix did a 10-for-1 stock split in November 2025).

With the stock trading near $58 as of this writing, a price in the high $30s would cut about another third off the shares.

I don't think investors have to wait for that. At today's price, Netflix already looks cheap for a company whose profits are still climbing.

Michael Burry. Image source: Getty Images.

Burry has been circling Netflix for months. In July, he figured that Netflix at $67.75 per share traded at about 1.6 times its intrinsic value, which comes out to a value of roughly $42 a share by his math. He also said then that the stock would have to drop to the low $50s to get closer to an ideal buying opportunity.

So, the high $30s isn't a random number. It would let him buy under his own estimate of what the company is worth.

His concern is how long Netflix's content keeps its value. In July, he compared the streaming giant to Walt Disney and its Pixar studio, whose content he called evergreen. Much of what Netflix makes, he suggested, is OK for now but doesn't improve with age.

Sure, that's a fair worry about any business built on hit shows. But it's a long-term debate. In the meantime, Netflix's profits keep growing, making the high $30s look like a really pessimistic price.

Call the high $30s $38 a share. On average, analysts expect Netflix to earn about $3.80 per share in 2027, so a $38 stock would cost about 10 times next year's expected earnings. At around $58, shares trade at about 15 times next year's expected earnings.

By comparison, the S&P 500 (^GSPC -3.70%) traded at 17.7 times its expected earnings for the next 12 months on Nov. 5, according to FactSet.

Said another way, Netflix stock already costs less than the average stock relative to its earnings. Burry's price would put it at a discount of over 40% to the market's price-to-earnings ratio.

For $38 to be a market-average price, Netflix would have to earn only about $2.15 per share. It earned $2.53 per share in 2025 (split-adjusted too), and its earnings per share have grown year over year in every quarter of 2026 so far.

The high $30s would only make sense, then, if Netflix's profits were about to shrink.

They don't look close to shrinking. Netflix's operating margin was 26.7% in 2024 and 29.5% in 2025, and the business is targeting 31.5% for 2026. Its third-quarter operating margin hit 34.6%. And management is guiding for a 26% margin in the fourth quarter, above the 24.5% Netflix reported a year before, even with costs from newly announced job cuts. All told, Netflix says its outlook implies operating income growth of over 20% this year.

Also, Netflix keeps returning cash to shareholders. It spent $4.8 billion buying back its own stock in the third quarter alone, and it still had $22.3 billion in repurchase authorizations left.

Sales growth, though, keeps slowing. Revenue growth has fallen every quarter this year, to 11.8% year over year in the third quarter from 16.2% in the first. And management expects around 11.5% for the fourth quarter, while it has lowered its 2026 ad revenue outlook to about $2.7 billion from about $3 billion.

But a business still growing sales by double digits while raising its operating margin looks unlikely to wind up earning less than it did in 2025.

Could Burry still get his price? He might. Netflix stock is already down over half from its June 2025 record, and a stock that's fallen this far can keep falling.

But waiting for the high $30s is arguably a bet that Netflix's earnings stall. At around 15 times next year's expected earnings, Netflix stock already prices in a lot of caution, I think. I'd consider buying shares now rather than waiting for Burry's number.

SBA Microloans – Up to $50,000 From Nonprofit Lenders When Banks Say No

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Many or all of the credit card, banking, and other offers on this site are from companies that pay us. For example, we may be paid when you click a link, are approved for a product, or open an account. Compensation may influence which products we cover and how, where, and in what order they appear. It doesn’t influence our editorial evaluations, ratings, or opinions, which are ours alone. We don’t include every bank, credit card company, or offer available in the marketplace.

If a bank turned your business down, apply for an SBA microloan of up to $50,000 from a nonprofit lender on the SBA’s list of microlenders. These lenders also coach borrowers on marketing and management. The money can buy equipment, inventory, or supplies, but it can’t pay off debt or buy real estate.

The SBA funds these lenders but doesn’t review your credit. Each lender decides who qualifies and sets your rate and terms.

A microloan tops out at $50,000, carries a fixed rate, and must be repaid within seven years. The SBA says rates usually run 8% to 13%. The business can be brand new, but it must be a for-profit small business in the U.S. Since April 2026, an SBA policy also requires every owner to be a U.S. citizen or national whose main home is in the U.S., which rules out green card holders.

SBA rules let a lender go past $20,000 only after you show that the business can’t get credit elsewhere at a comparable rate and that it’s likely to succeed. A bank’s rejection in writing helps you show the first.

The money can also serve as working capital, the cash that runs the business day to day, or pay for furniture, fixtures, and machinery.

Say a mobile dog groomer turned down by her bank borrows $18,000 at a fixed 9% over five years to outfit a van. Her payment would be about $374 a month, and she’d pay about $4,420 in interest over the loan.

The coaching can start before you borrow. Lenders can spend up to half of their SBA coaching grants on people who haven’t borrowed yet.

Find lenders on the SBA’s list of microlenders, filtered to your state, and call two or three for help with your application. Ask each for its rate, what property it would hold as collateral, and whether you’d sign a personal guarantee, which makes you personally responsible for the loan.

For a loan outside the microloan program, compare the best small business loans and SBA lenders or the nonprofit lender Accion Opportunity Fund.

7 Classic Fantasy Books That Are Even Bigger Today

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Beatriz (Bea) Valero de Urquia is a Books & Literature author at Collider, where she focuses on Lists surrounding recent bestsellers, classical novels, and everything in between. She is a historian by training and a bilingual reader, moving between English and Spanish literature.   Beatriz began her career as a technology writer at a startup in the UK. Almost a decade later, journalism has taken her to five cities across three continents and into business reporting, cultural criticism, and live TV commentary. She joined Collider in 2026.  Beatriz has a passion for travelling and loves keeping an eye on the books headed to the screen. One of her favorite pastimes is critiquing the historical accuracy of period movies. She also once volunteered for the Charles Dickens Museum. Beatriz also has a core belief that a book is always better than the movie, but she has been happy to have been proven wrong in a couple of occasions.

When she isn’t reading, Beatriz is watching musicals, stage managing Shakespeare plays with a local theater group or doing historical research. She enjoys tracing family trees for her history and genealogy TikTok, creating databases for historical analysis and sifting through 17th-century stories for the novel she is currently in the process of writing.

Just like our favorite characters, some books need time to find their place in the world. The author might not be well known, or perhaps they were born in a non-English-speaking country. Some books are so long they can be intimidating, particularly when they introduce readers to a complex magical system. But when given the chance — through word of mouth, a movie adaptation, or just the passing of time — great books bloom.

The books on this list are far greater today than they were when they were first published. Most were never truly obscure, but they are or have been cultural phenomena in a way that few fantasy stories are able to achieve. They are classics that have stood the test of time, as new generations grow into them. If you are a fan of fantasy novels, it is likely that the reason lies in one of these series.

Terry Pratchett was the top-selling and highest-earning author in the UK in 1996. It is therefore hard to imagine a time when his novels didn’t sell all that well. When the author’s publisher, Colin Smythe Ltd, released the first novel in what would become the Discworld series, it only printed 506 copies, the majority going to libraries. The US printed about 4,000 copies, and the author suffered poorer sales in North America until the early 2000s.

The first Discworld book follows the adventures of the hopeless wizard Rincewind, who is forced to be a guide for Twoflower and show him a flat planet balanced on the backs of four elephants standing on a giant turtle. From that description alone, one grasps the type of humor Pratchett uses throughout, and what makes it so brilliant. The main characters’ contrasting personalities also carry the story on their backs. "Twoflower was a tourist, the first ever seen on the discworld,” the book says. “Tourist, Rincewind had decided, meant 'idiot'."

What happens when an author dies before finishing his best-selling, fan-adored fantasy book series? In this case, another would-be best-selling, fan-adored fantasy book author picks up the pen and does the job. The writer chosen to complete Robert Jordan’s The Wheel of Time series, which began with The Eye of the World, was Brandon Sanderson. Prime Video’s TV show adaptation also helped propel its popularity and introduced it to a new generation of readers, 30 years after the book’s release.

Rand, Perrin, and Matrim are just three young men living in the small village of Emond’s Field. The possibility of being the Dragon Reborn is the furthest thing they could ever imagine. And yet, when a mysterious woman, a member of the powerful Aes Sedai, arrives at the village, and shortly after it is attacked by Trollocs, the possibility starts to become more likely. The three of them then agree to leave alongside their friend Egwene. Their travels will see them separate and reunite, as the four characters struggle to learn who they are and how to fight the Dark One.

If there is a living titan of the fantasy genre, that would be Brandon Sanderson. In addition to completing The Wheel of Time series, Sanderson has written over 60 books, including roughly 30 novels, alongside numerous novellas, short stories, and graphic novels. Most of them are set in the Cosmere universe. More impressively, he has become an undisputed best-selling author in the US and abroad, and it all started with Mistborn.

The premise for Mistborn: The Final Empire was simple yet innovative: What happens if the villain wins the war? The book is set in the world of Scadrial, within the Cosmere universe. At the start of the story, the Final Empire is ruled by the Lord Ruler. It is a society where humans are divided into nobility and the oppressed skaa, and certain people called Allomancers are able to use magic by ingesting certain metals. When the half-skaa Kelsier and his crew find Vin, a street urchin with Allomantic powers, and develop a plan to rob the empire, the regime’s foundations start to shake.

Before it was Studio Ghibli’s beloved 2004 animated movie, Howl's Moving Castle was an 18-year-old children’s fantasy book, written by the British author Diana Wynne Jones. In a note at the front of the 1986 novel, Jones explains that it was a child at a school she was visiting who asked her to write a book about a moving castle. The Oscar-nominated movie pushed the story into the limelight and sent a wave of readers searching for more information about their lives and adventures.

The story follows Sophie, a young woman who is turned into a crone by the powerful Witch of the Waste. Embarrassed, she leaves her home and gets employment as the cleaning lady for the famous wizard Howl, who lives in a mechanical moving castle. It is the same premise as that of the Japanese adaptation. However, in the novel, everything is turned up. Book Howl is more of a coward, while Sophie is wittier. Moreover, the plot takes a wild detour into modern-day Wales that Miyazaki dropped entirely.

Meet Geralt of Rivia. If you don’t know him already, he is a witcher, a magical monster hunter who travels across the world killing dangerous creatures for money. In The Last Wish, Geralt is recovering from battle wounds in a temple, remembering several recent events from his life, such as the time he acted as a bodyguard at a royal banquet that ended up in a surprise engagement, or when he was asked to lift a king’s daughter’s curse, which turned her into a vicious striga.

The Last Wish is a collection of fantasy short stories written by Andrzej Sapkowski and published in the early 1990s. For the first decade after its publication, Geralt of Rivia’s fame was contained within Central and Eastern Europe. The short story collection was only translated into English in 2007. It became a worldwide phenomenon after it was adapted into a video game and, later, became a hit Netflix series originally starring Henry Cavill. Today, CD Projekt Red's The Witcher 3: Wild Hunt has sold more than 60 million copies, making Geralt one of gaming's most recognizable faces.

"Are people born wicked? Or do they have wickedness thrust upon them?" This is the question that Glinda the Good Witch poses at the start of the Tony Award-winning musical Wicked, which tells the story of Elphaba and how she became the Wicked Witch of the West. The musical is a loose (and very sanitized) adaptation of Gregory Maguire's novel Wicked, itself a prequel to L. Frank Baum's 1900 children's novel The Wonderful Wizard of Oz. The book was published to mixed reviews and modest sales.

However, the musical and, later, Jon M. Chu’s two-part movie adaptation starring Cynthia Erivo and Ariana Grande made the novel a bestseller. Gregory Maguire’s version of Elphaba’s story is dark and very adult. While the musical and Baum’s original story are beloved by children, Maguire’s take is densely detailed, intensely political, very cynical, and much more sexual. Glinda has a much smaller role to play, and there is little of the whimsy of Baum’s world. For those ready to delve into a story about the nature of evil and political radicalization set in a fantasy world, it is a fascinating and worthwhile read.

The first book in the series that created phrases like “winter is coming” or “sweet summer child” barely sold a few thousand copies upon release. It seems unthinkable today, but for 15 years, A Song of Ice and Fire was known mostly within fantasy book circles. George R.R. Martin's book signings drew few fans of the story that follows Ned Stark’s family and the power struggle for the Iron Throne in a history-inspired fantasy realm. It was not until HBO turned it into a TV show that it became a worldwide hit.

Fans are still waiting for The Winds of Winter and the conclusion to the book series, but the story that started it all has never been bigger. Despite its divisive final season, the show is still considered one of the best of all time, earning 59 Primetime Emmy Awards, spurring two prequel shows (House of the Dragon and A Knight of the Seven Kingdoms), and drawing millions of fans across the world to pick up a fantasy book for perhaps the first time.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

Steelers need more from DK Metcalf, other highly paid players

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The Pittsburgh Steelers are 6-3 and still well positioned to make the NFL playoffs because of a favorable schedule.

For that reason, the 38-18 loss to the Cincinnati Bengals on Nov. 15 was not a total disaster and could plausibly be written off as just a bad day against a red-hot offense led by leading MVP candidate Joe Burrow.

At least that might be true if the team's most highly paid players were producing at the levels they're paid to. They decidedly are not, and until they are, then the Steelers still profile as an upper middle class AFC team capable of reaching the postseason, but probably not winning once they get there.

Required reading: Steelers' veteran CB was torched by Joe Burrow in loss to Bengals: PFF grades

Receiver DK Metcalf, who's in Year 2 of a five-year, $150 million deal, is the chief offender. Through nine games, he's on pace for the least productive season of his eight-year NFL career. It would break the lower threshold that he established last just year, his first in Pittsburgh, when he had 850 yards.

Pro Football Focus data faults him for six drops, a figure that feels generous given how frequently he fails to come down with catchable balls from quarterback Aaron Rodgers.

The contested catch numbers encapsulate the profound struggles better. PFF credits him with receptions on just 10 of 25 opportunities in 2026. That's a 40% contested catch rate, which is the worst in the NFL among receivers with at least that many opportunities. Only eight out of 38 receivers with at least 50 targets are worse overall.

Within that cohort, Metcalf ranks 33rd in yards per route run at 1.24; 32nd in yards after the catch with 125; and dead last in raw catch percentage when targeted at 50.7%.

By no metric has he been the reliable No. 1 option that he's being paid to be. Granted, it's certainly not helped that key offseason acquisition Michael Pittman Jr. hasn't been around much to draw coverage to the opposite side of the field. Still, for $150 million, you expect a player to be able to transcend his circumstances more often than Metcalf has.

He finished the game against the Bengals with 54 yards and a touchdown but was blanked before the game got out of hand. He simply hasn't been good enough.

Linebacker/pass rusher T.J. Watt is also worthy of scrutiny. In two games against Cincinnati this season, the Steelers' $42 million man this season generated zero pressures on Burrow. Not just zero sacks. No hurries, and no quarterback hits. Needless to say, that's not good when Burrow is the man he's primarily meant to disrupt.

Four of his eight sacks this season came in the first two games. And he's on pace for the fewest tackles of his career with nine total so far, meaning he's contributing little unless he's getting to the quarterback.

There are certainly games that he's taken over. His interception return for a touchdown sealed the Week 1 win against Atlanta. His two sacks against Cleveland in Week 8 helped give his team the outright division lead momentarily.

PFF, however, has graded him at 63.8/100 or worse in five of his last seven games. The consistency is nowhere close to where it was when he was winning Defensive Player of the Year honors and setting the NFL sack record.

Beyond those two, you can find fault in a lot of the more highly paid players' respective games.

Tight end Pat Freiermuth has often been invisible as a receiving target with Pittman out because of injury. He's caught just two passes in each of the past four games, and his production against Cincinnati came mostly at garbage time.

Pittman, obviously, hasn't been available much at all because of his foot injuries. He's the offense's third-highest paid player behind Metcalf and Rodgers and has just seven catches for 89 yards in three games.

Linebacker Alex Highsmith, has just one half sack in the past seven games. His cap hit is $17 million in 2026. That problem compounds itself, too, because that lack of production will likely hurt his trade value in the offseason, when the Steelers might be looking to unload him because of big commitments to Watt and fellow outside backer Nick Herbig.

And Rodgers himself was not sharp at all against Cincinnati. He didn't complete a pass for most of the first half. He'd thrown just two touchdowns in regulation in the previous three games. And his two scoring tosses against the Bengals came once the game was well out of reach. He's in a definitive slump himself.

Add it all up, and the Steelers are relying more heavily on their depth than they probably should be in Year 1 of the transition to Mike McCarthy as coach.

If this franchise is going to win its first playoff game since 2016 come January, the guys making the most need to begin playing like it. Otherwise, another disappointment like Sunday night's could be the start of a long winter once again.

Why Delta Air Lines Quietly Added ETOPS Certification To Its Boeing 737-900ERs Even Though They Don't Fly Transatlantic & Pacific Legs

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

In 2011, Delta Air Lines announced an order for 100 Boeing 737-900ERs, the largest variant of the 737 family. It later ordered another 20 units in 2015 and another 10 in 2017, bringing its total to 130, and the airline acquired another 33 second-hand aircraft after COVID. They first started arriving in 2013, and the large, efficient 737-900ER has become a workhorse for Delta's domestic network. The type, however, isn't known for exceptional range and flies only within North America. Yet, these aircraft are certified for ETOPS-180 operations.

ETOPS stands for Extended-range Twin-engine Operational Performance Standards (or alternatively, Engines Turn Or Passengers Swim), and ETOPS certification demonstrates that a twin-engine aircraft may safely fly more than 60 minutes from a diversion airfield. The number denotes how far the aircraft may fly from an airfield in minutes. Although the 737-900ER does not operate any transoceanic flights, Delta has chosen to maintain ETOPS certification on some of its 737-900ERs, but not all of them.

Modern twinjets are certified by regulators with an ETOPS type design approval, but this doesn't mean that a specific aircraft is ETOPS-certified. Planes need to be individually approved for ETOPS operations, as the regulator needs to verify that the aircraft features special equipment mandatory for overwater operations and that the airline has procedures in place that comply with ETOPS practices. An airline could even choose to have just one aircraft certified for ETOPS, or it could certify its entire fleet if desired.

The ETOPS certification process is expensive, and the extra equipment adds weight to the aircraft. Therefore, airlines generally only certify aircraft with ETOPS if the aircraft will regularly operate overwater flights to make the investment worth it. As an example, all of Delta's widebodies are ETOPS certified, but many of its short-haul aircraft are not. Notably, however, the 737-900ER lacks the range to operate transoceanic routes and is primarily used for domestic services.

But although the 737-900ER is not generally known for having long range, the aircraft is still capable of operating longer services. This includes transcontinental flights or services to the Caribbean.

While ETOPS is not generally necessary for Caribbean flights, it can permit more efficient routings in some cases, and the 737-900ER is still commonly deployed on the carrier's routes to this region. In addition, while the role of the 737-900ER has been changing as Delta receives a higher number of new narrowbody aircraft, the carrier formerly had a majority of its 737-900ERs ETOPS certified.

Today, Delta only maintains a small subfleet of roughly 20 737-900ERs with ETOPS certification, while the remaining are not certified for extended overwater operations. However, many of these planes formerly featured equipment for ETOPS operations and were maintained to ETOPS-180 standards. Delta later removed the extra equipment from many of its 737s and no longer holds them to ETOPS standards.

The 737-900ER was Delta's newest and most economical large narrowbody in the mid-2010s. With such a large order, the Atlanta-based carrier intended to deploy the 737-900ER throughout its network on some of its longest routes within North America. Some of these are Caribbean services that would require ETOPS certification for more efficient routings; hence, most of the 737-900ERs got ETOPS certification. In addition, Delta also originally intended to use the 737-900ER for some of its services to Hawaii from the West Coast, which necessitates ETOPS-180.

ETOPS equipment and maintaining ETOPS certification for an aircraft is expensive, so Delta has removed it from most 737s as the airline uses the 737 on fewer overwater routes today. Part of this is because the carrier now has a large Airbus A321neo fleet, which has been deployed on longer Caribbean routes due to its excellent fuel efficiency. The other reason is that the previously planned Hawaii services didn't work out, and Delta pulled the 737s from these operations.

Southwest Airlines has just 138 ETOPS-certified aircraft in its fleet of over 800 aircraft.

Until the arrival of the 737-900ER, Delta primarily relied on the far less efficient 757 for its routes from the West Coast to Hawaii. In 2016, the airline began scheduling the 737-900ER on flights between Seattle and Honolulu, while the 737 began flying to Kona shortly afterwards. However, the airline would end up pulling the 737-900ER off of these routes, instead replacing it with the 757 again, as these routes pushed the 737-900ER too far past its payload-range limitations.

The 737-900ER can and has operated longer routes than these Hawaii services, even for Delta itself. However, operating an ETOPS flight requires extra fuel reserves; in the case of the Hawaii routes, the aircraft needs to carry enough fuel to essentially turn around midway, as there are no diversion airports between the West Coast and Hawaii. The fuel reserves add significant weight, cutting into payload, and the 737-900ERs were frequently payload-restricted when flying to Hawaii, hurting revenue.

United Airlines also faced this same issue when operating the 737-900ER to Hawaii, but the Chicago-based carrier had a smaller 757 fleet than Delta, and most of its examples were configured with lie-flat business class seats. Delta, with its large subfleets of domestic-configured 757s, was able to switch the routes back to the 757s, which cost more to operate but also earned more revenue. Today, Delta mainly relies on the A321neo for its Hawaii routes, while United relies on the 737 MAX, neither of which faces these issues.

Delta Air Lines uses the A321neo, 737-900ER, and 757 for routes to the Caribbean and Hawaii. Because its network of overwater routes within North America is operated by several types, the airline maintains subfleets of planes with ETOPS certification and planes without. Only about half of the A321neos are certified for ETOPS operations, as well as select 737s and 757s. As a whole, just over 100 of Delta's 828 narrowbodies are certified for ETOPS, because this is all that the airline needs.

United Airlines, however, certifies all of its 640 737s and 61 757s for ETOPS operations. The 737s are the airline's primary aircraft for Caribbean and Hawaii routes, while the 757-200 is heavily used to Europe. The 757-300s are also still used on several of United's flights to Hawaii. Still, the carrier has significantly more ETOPS-certified aircraft than it likely needs; it even maintains ETOPS certification on its 12 737-900s, which mainly operate short domestic trips out of Houston. This is a costlier approach, but what it allows is operational flexibility.

If an ETOPS-certified 737 goes out of service, United can just swap it for another 737 without complicating the rest of its schedule. If one of Delta's ETOPS-certified 737s is taken out of service, the carrier has to swap it out for another ETOPS-certified 737, of which it has far fewer. Delta is maintaining separate subfleets of A321neos, 737s, and 757s, which is a less flexible arrangement regarding operational disruptions and spares. By certifying all of its 737s with ETOPS, United avoids this problem.

United doesn't maintain ETOPS certification on its A319-100, A320-200, or A321neo fleets, and so it essentially designates one aircraft type (the 737) for Caribbean and Hawaii runs, while the A320 family is restricted to the US mainland. The catch is that this approach is more expensive upfront.

The A350 has the highest ETOPS rating of 370, meaning it can fly up to six hours and 10 minutes from the nearest usable landing strip.

ETOPS certification is not generally required for flights from the US to the Caribbean. However, there are some airways southeast of Bermuda to the Eastern Caribbean that take an aircraft more than 60 minutes away from a diversion airfield, and if Bermuda is unavailable as a diversion point, then planes need to hug the Florida coastline. While ETOPS certification is not necessary for Caribbean flights, it does offer benefits through more direct flight paths, and it is necessary for Hawaii operations.

To actually be certified for ETOPS operations, an aircraft needs to be maintained according to a special ETOPS program. It needs to be equipped with additional communications and navigation equipment, and an ETOPS-qualified mechanic is required to complete a service check before the flight. The engines must be inspected by two separate technicians, and the aircraft's maintenance logs also denote it specifically as being ETOPS-certified.

ETOPS is meant to be a thorough process that ensures a twinjet can safely fly far away from a diversion point due to having only two engines. Because it's expensive, airlines generally only certify aircraft with ETOPS if the plane will routinely be used on overwater routes. Even if a route doesn't necessarily require it, ETOPS can offer better routings that save time and fuel. Today, 737s and A321s now routinely operate overwater routes to Hawaii or across oceans, and this would have been unimaginable 40 years ago.

"ETOPS flight requires extra fuel reserves; in the case of the Hawaii routes, the aircraft needs to carry enough fuel to essentially turn around midway, as there are no diversion airports between the West Coast and Hawaii."

Mariska Hargitay Made Her Acting Debut In A Cult Favorite '80s Horror Movie

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

We may receive a commission on purchases made from links.

Mariska Hargitay has had one heck of a career as an actor. She'll forever be known as Detective Olivia Benson, the best "Law and Order: SVU" character of all time, and has, remarkably, appeared in several hundred episodes of the show. But decades before she joined the "Law & Order" franchise, she made her feature film debut in a cult 1980s horror movie.

Directed by Luca Bercovici, "Ghoulies" was released in 1984. It came out mere months after the stone-cold '80s classic that is "Gremlins" ruled the box office. Now, nobody can say that "Ghoulies" was as successful or as well-liked as "Gremlins," but releasing a film about little chaotic creatures around that time was undoubtedly a recipe for success.

The movie follows a young man and his girlfriend as they move into his parents' old mansion, where his satanic father is buried. When the young man performs a ceremony described in one of his father's black magic books, he unwittingly unleashes small, demonic creatures. Hargitay plays Donna, one of the guests at the house who encounters these little monsters.

"Ghoulies" is often accused of being a "Gremlins" rip-off, but, in actuality, the movies were competing to see which could make it to theaters first. In a 2018 interview with Love-It-Loud, Bercovici explained how it all went down.

"We were in production at the same time. We were curious about them, and they were curious about us. They were so curious about us, in fact, that Warner Bros. briefly sued to stop us from using the name — they lost. Part of the reason that 'Gremlins' came out first, is that Charlie Band ran out of money halfway through shooting, so a few months went by as we all scrambled for the money, allowing 'Gremlins' to open first."

While "Gremlins" had producer Steven Spielberg, director Joe Dante, and critics on its side, "Ghoulies" did not. Instead, it holds a terrible 7% critical approval rating on Rotten Tomatoes. But horror has never relied on critical opinion for success, particularly in the '80s. As a result, Mariska Hargitay's feature debut became a sizable hit.

Released in January 1985 and produced for a budget of just a few hundred thousand dollars, "Ghoulies" did big business (as much as $35 million at the box office, by some accounts). That paved the way for a string of sequels, including 1987's "Ghoulies II," 1991's "Ghoulies III: Ghoulies Go to College," and 1994's "Ghoulies IV." Good or bad, it made no difference. These movies were very successful.

Mind you, it's not remotely uncommon for well-known actors to get their start in less-than-prestigious horror movies. Oscar-winner Leonardo DiCaprio made his feature film debut in the 1990s creature feature "Critters 3," while Jennifer Aniston, who later broke out on "Friends," similarly made an early career appearance in "Leprechaun." It happens all the time.

Interestingly enough, Hargitay auditioned many times for a lead role on "Friends," only to be denied. She was also fired from the 1995 "Power Rangers" movie. The early '90s were not terribly kind to Hargitay. But she eventually made her way to "SVU" and, it almost goes without saying, that worked out pretty darn well for her.

You can grab "Ghoulies" on 4K, Blu-ray, or DVD from Amazon.

8 Places to Buy Shoes Online for Less – What a Return Costs at Each

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Many or all of the credit card, banking, and other offers on this site are from companies that pay us. For example, we may be paid when you click a link, are approved for a product, or open an account. Compensation may influence which products we cover and how, where, and in what order they appear. It doesn’t influence our editorial evaluations, ratings, or opinions, which are ours alone. We don’t include every bank, credit card company, or offer available in the marketplace.

Discount sites, shoe chains’ online clearance sections, and brands’ own sale pages sell brand-name shoes for less, but the return terms decide whether a bad fit costs you. Before you order a size you haven’t worn, check the return window and the cost of a mailed return, since several sellers charge $8.50 to $9.99.

Shoes in the same size fit differently from one brand to the next, and even between models from one brand, so a pair ordered online is a guess until the box arrives.

Return terms split the eight sellers below into three groups. Three pay for a mailed return, though one does so only for members and another only in the lower 48 states. Three more take returns free in their stores but deduct $8.50 or $9.95 from a refund by mail, and the last two make you pay for every return you mail.

Here are the eight, from the friendliest return terms to the costliest:

The sellers with free mail returns make it cheap to order two sizes and keep one. Say you’re between a 10 and a 10.5 in a $140 running shoe from Running Warehouse. Order both, try them on a clean floor, keep the pair that fits, and send the other back with the prepaid label from the box. You pay $280 up front, $140 comes back once the refund posts, and the return costs nothing. From a seller that deducts $9.95 by mail, the same order at the same price leaves the pair you keep costing $149.95.

Keep any try-on indoors, since most of these sellers refund only unworn pairs. Zappos also wants its shoe box back without tape or postal labels, so pack it inside a shipping box.

Buy a size you’re unsure of from Zappos, Running Warehouse, or Nike as a member, or from DSW, Famous Footwear, or Nordstrom Rack when one of their stores is near you. Save 6pm and Joe’s New Balance Outlet for a model you already own in the right size.

In a 32-year managerial career he won 13 league titles, having success with both Linfield and Glentoran while also taking charge of Ards, Derry City and Newry City.

As a player, he won five caps for Northern Ireland and represented Ballymena, Glentoran, Sheffield Wednesday, Grimsby and Linfield.

Irish FA president Colin McKendry said: “Roy was an icon of our game and someone who was ever-present throughout my time involved in football.

“The way that we work is indifferent to the title description,” Pochettino said. “It depends on the structure, depends on the communication, the relationship with all the areas. I know that being a head coach I am the guy that is going to decide tomorrow the squad for Sunday and I’m going to pick the starting XI. I’m going to make the changes, and of course in the future I would like to be involved in all the main decisions that will happen on the squad. In the end, Manchester United is a football club.”

Is Markel Group Stock a Buy After Falling 10% in 2026?

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Markel stock has been surging in recent weeks.

By Dave Kovaleski – Nov 21, 2026 at 6:45AM EST

10 stocks we like better than Markel Group ›

After falling to a 52-week low of $1,704 per share on Oct. 1, Markel Group (MKL -0.77%) stock has rallied, rising about 15% to $1,952 per share (as of Nov. 19), though it’s still down about 10% for the year.

The specialty insurer and holding company operates a model that is not unlike Berkshire Hathaway (BRKB -0.36%). In fact, some have referred to it as a baby Berkshire Hathaway. Like Berkshire, Markel is mainly an insurance company that uses its float — the premiums it receives before paying out claims — to invest in a portfolio of stocks and privately owned businesses.

Markel has also not been as successful as Berkshire Hathaway, at least not in recent years. It has averaged a 9.2% annualized return during the past five years compared to Berkshire's 15.7% average return. During the past 10 years, Markel stock has posted an average return of 8.5% to 14.1% for Berkshire.

But like Berkshire, there is a lot to like about Markel.

You can't gauge Markel's quarter by looking at traditional earnings based on generally accepted accounting principles (GAAP) because the unrealized investment gains and losses from its portfolio are included. Those unrealized gains or losses distort the actual performance of the company.

But if you look at its adjusted operating income, that shows how much the company made on the businesses it owns. In Q3, its adjusted operating income rose 14% from a year ago to $707 million, or $37.51 per share. That soundly beat analysts' estimates of $27.76 per share. Its revenue jumped 6% year-over-year to $4.16 billion, which easily beat estimates of $3.78 billion.

Within insurance, Markel saw its adjusted underwriting gross premium volume increase by 9% for the quarter. This excludes its global reinsurance division, which it exited in the past year, and its specialty car insurance business Hagerty, which transitioned to a fronting arrangement. Also, its net investment income increased 5% to $233 million.

The key result in insurance is the combined ratio, which measures how much the company makes in profits from premiums after claims are paid out and operating expenses are subtracted. Markel had a combined ratio of 92%, down from 93% a year ago.

For the full year, the combined ratio is 92%, down from 95% in 2026. This is a good thing because a combined ratio of more than 100 means you are paying out more than you are taking in. A ratio below 100 means you are making more than you are paying out. So, a combined ratio of 92% means Markel is making more profit per dollar of premiums than it was last year.

Markel's businesses, known as Markel Ventures, also performed well. Its industrial holdings saw operating income rise 29% year-over-year to $131 million, while its financial segment increased 30% to $80 million and its consumer properties rose 80% to $31 million.

Markel doesn't provide short-term projections, but its trends within insurance and its holdings are promising. The company is built to perform well in negative markets, which is a big reason the shares surged in recent weeks.

When investors make a flight to safety, they head for companies like Markel, which is grounded by its insurance holdings and conservative underwriting and its durable, value-oriented investments with low debt and stable earnings.

The stock is also fairly cheap right now, trading at less than 14 times earnings. I like it as a long-term buy, and it's particularly valuable now during bouts of market volatility.
